## Introduction

pgBackRest release numbers consist of three parts: major, minor, and patch. A major release *may* break compatibility with the prior major release, but v2 releases are fully compatible with v1 repositories and will accept all v1 options. Minor releases can include bug fixes and features but do not change the repository format and strive to avoid changing options and naming. Patch releases contain bug fixes for the most recent minor release.

Feature releases are scheduled for the third Monday of January, April, July, and October. Patch releases are scheduled for the third Monday of any other month as required.

Documentation for the v1 release can be found [here](https://pgbackrest.org/1).

The notes for a release may also contain "Additional Notes" but changes in this section are only to documentation or the test suite and have no direct impact on the pgBackRest codebase.

--------

## Documented Stable Release {#current-stable-release}

This mirror covers v2.60.0. See the [upstream release history](https://pgbackrest.org/release.html) for newer versions.

### v2.60.0 Release Notes {#v2600-release-notes}

*Page Checksum Errors and Object Store Prefetch*

*Released October 18, 2026*

**IMPORTANT NOTE**: The `restore` command will now error and stop if any restored file contains an invalid page checksum, which leaves the cluster incomplete and unable to start, so the restore must be rerun. The `expire` command will now refuse to expire if the oldest retained backup would contain checksum errors, which also prevents archive from being expired in that repository. Set `checksum-page-error=n` to warn instead of erroring.

**Features:**

- Add `checksum-page-error` option to error on invalid page checksums. (*Contributed by Andrew Pogrebnoi. Reviewed by Douglas J Hunley, David Steele, Greg Sabino Mullane, Stefan Fercot. Suggested by Greg Sabino Mullane.*)

  This option applies to the `backup`, `restore`, and `expire` commands. The `backup` command defaults to a warning for each file (as before) and now also reports the total number of checksum warnings at the end of the backup. The `restore` and `expire` commands default to an error. Previously the `restore` command did not report invalid page checksums at all.

  Issue [#2687](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2687); Pull request [#2833](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2833); Commit [`d7f54b2`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/d7f54b2245c8a9467e9a811c65626fe77739376b)
- Support for GCS Workload Identity Federation. (*Contributed by Adol Rodriguez. Reviewed by Douglas J Hunley, David Steele.*)

  Set `repo-gcs-key-type=web-id` and set `repo-gcs-key` to an external account credential file, e.g. as generated by `gcloud iam workload-identity-pools create-cred-config`. The web identity token is exchanged for an access token without a service account key. The token file is reloaded on each exchange to pick up rotated tokens, e.g. Kubernetes projected service account tokens. Service account impersonation is not supported.

  Issue [#2838](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2838); Pull request [#2839](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2839); Commit [`1d217e6`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/1d217e626301118ae2c1e7be39a6a4affa86e501)
- Prefetch multi-file/range reads from object stores. (*Reviewed by Andrew Pogrebnoi, Douglas J Hunley.*)

  Requests are queued ahead of the current read so requests proceed while reading, and small gaps between files are read over instead of starting a new request. `Restore` uses this to read bundled files and block incremental maps, and `backup` uses it to read block incremental maps from the prior backup. `repo-storage-prefetch` (default 3) sets how many requests are queued and `repo-storage-read-over` (default 64KiB) sets the largest gap that is read over.

  [`e63dec3`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/e63dec323b9e79513638b9858e4ad13b6072d060) Prefetch multi-file/range reads from object stores.; Pull request [#2783](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2783)

  [`5ae4015`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/5ae4015d37081eba4259f4de379a9d160236de0e) Combine prior block incremental map reads during backup.; Pull request [#2816](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2816)

**Improvements:**

- Reduce keep-alive requests to remote processes. (*Reviewed by Douglas J Hunley.*)

  A keep-alive was sent to every remote each time a job completed in `archive-push` and `backup`, even when the remotes had just been sent a request. A keep-alive is now sent only when no request has been sent to the remote for 30 seconds.

  Commit [`f652261`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/f652261d32a4ffba98e7da26a41ef4ef1cd08da3)
- Retry Azure batch sub-requests missing from the response. (*Reviewed by Douglas J Hunley.*)

  When Azure fails a batch delete it may return a single response for the entire batch. The sub-requests with no response were treated as complete, so only one file per batch was removed while the command reported success. Sub-requests with no response are now retried.

  Issue [#2840](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2840); Pull request [#2849](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2849); Commit [`567a245`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/567a2451244e0e7a4c54e41880fac1a007b49930)
- Reduce the manifest checks made when reconstructing `backup.info`. (*Reviewed by Douglas J Hunley. Suggested by todeb.*)

  The `backup`, `expire`, and `annotate` commands no longer check for a `backup.manifest` when the repository listing shows the backup path does not exist, or when a diff or incr backup belongs to a full backup set that is not in `backup.info`. Each check is a request so on an object store the check for a missing manifest is logged as a 404.

  Issue [#2769](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2769); Pull request [#2825](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2825); Commit [`0d3c340`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/0d3c340087bfb97263c29a8fd681cf079071236b)
- Use original size to determine if a file should be bundled. (*Reviewed by Douglas J Hunley.*)

  The size of a block incremental file in the prior backup was used to decide whether to bundle it, so a file that had grown past `repo-bundle-limit` could still be stored in a bundle. The current size of the file is now used.

  Pull request [#2843](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2843); Commit [`340d58b`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/340d58bb49d6c88a002a0d23830fd37be155cc21)
- Add context to block map read errors during backup. (*Reviewed by Stefan Fercot, Douglas J Hunley.*)

  An error reading the block map of a prior backup now reports the file, repository path, offset, and map size. A read that returns fewer bytes than expected is reported as such instead of as a parse error.

  [`bc91688`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/bc91688c4cf18fa6a36ec65441784533d5e6bdd5) Add context to block map read errors during backup.; Issue [#2489](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2489); Issue [#2829](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2829); Pull request [#2830](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2830)

  [`f46bfaf`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/f46bfafdd53dd0031a320fa95b08568263901ff3) Fix option name in block incremental map errors.
- Cast `current_timestamp` to `text` in the backup start label. (*Contributed by Tiago Corcelli de Oliveira. Reviewed by David Steele.*)

  A user-defined implicit cast from `timestamptz` to `text` caused the backup to fail with "operator is not unique: unknown || timestamp with time zone". The label text is unchanged.

  Issue [#2873](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2873); Commit [`86936e1`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/86936e1fa60b10b7592eaac09283c4bb5991265d)
- Support building on illumos. (*Contributed by Kalevi Kolttonen. Reviewed by Douglas J Hunley, David Steele.*)

  An OmniOS job has been added to CI. Solaris may also work but has not been tested.

  Pull request [#2870](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2870); Commit [`ad72e58`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/ad72e5815f3cf9c0c6170dcbe04ef9c41762d3d9)
- Support OpenSSL 4. (*Reviewed by Douglas J Hunley.*)

  Building against OpenSSL 4 failed because `X509_get_subject_name()` now returns a `const` name.

  Commit [`a729ac8`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/a729ac881f48225622151841e80146f7a3a4bce6)
- Fix clang builds. (*Reviewed by Douglas J Hunley.*)

  On Linux, gcc installs `libbacktrace.a` where clang also looks for it but installs `backtrace.h` where only gcc can find it. A clang build found the library and then failed to compile. The library is now only used when its header is found. Return types that clang reports as narrowing conversions in debug builds are also fixed.

  [`84b031a`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/84b031af68fe6a30b4150ec6c903cc8c1781fc05) Require the header when checking for libbacktrace.

  [`195e66b`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/195e66bafd89da511fb0d48e6e053176104c47c7) Return the declared type from functions that return a StringId-based enum.

  [`d9e5e14`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/d9e5e14e93f460d62930f58dd4dd5bbc2a9f3a85) Return bool from a function declared to return bool.
- Migrate code generation from C to Python.

  Code is generated during the build from the option, error, help, and PostgreSQL declarations by a Python tool instead of a C tool that had to be built first. Building from a git checkout requires PyYAML instead of libyaml. The release tarball includes the generated code and requires neither.

  Commit [`59bd2cd`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/59bd2cd96cfba2b02b07e81ade57d9beb5f2812e)

**Documentation Improvements:**

- Modernize the website. Special thanks to Jan for the insightful (and hilarious) logo. (*Contributed by David Steele, Jan Wieremjewicz. Reviewed by Steve Atkins, Kai Wagner, Douglas J Hunley, Nikolaos Stamatopoulos, Matt Hudson, Stefan Fercot.*)

  The website has a new design with light and dark themes and a layout that works on phones. Pages with a table of contents show it beside the text and mark the section being read. Commands and config files can be copied with a button, config files mark the lines each section changes, and release notes can be expanded to show more detail.

  [`5bc14b5`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/5bc14b581a054ab3dc9cd633954a689acc99a58f) Shorten and disambiguate the documentation titles.

  [`b668d71`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/b668d71f22489cbd4d5e120e7e6e73be0ca9e167) Move the sponsors down the index and shorten the appeal.

  [`60b85c5`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/60b85c58d98dd65d47747e6355f47c934cd6b430) Modernize the website.

  [`ab8df23`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/ab8df23807cc4c11768ef40196bcade70ebec23a) Show a card rather than the logo where a link is posted.

  [`76ae494`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/76ae49456c7dace20bbd6f17e8acdfd36bf03a46) Remove the contents from the releases page and close up the date.

  [`3e86c71`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/3e86c71f5546c86941f35d5be3a7fdcd98af3cca) Link rather than repeat the options in the command reference.

  [`dc3af7b`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/dc3af7b2aee5778dbe9200e31d5a2f95ecae5777) Clarify the command titles in the command reference.

  [`078d52c`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/078d52c5768bb67989624b5418fdd687fab9e485) Mark the lines a section changes in a config file.

  [`bd33623`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/bd33623ba945ed251e2a6704cea14a81d51c2c9e) Mark the output of a command the way a config file is marked.

  [`578fc2d`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/578fc2de14a6a739401fd58b9a232cf587c52fb4) Copy a command as well as a config file.

  [`1fb93f9`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/1fb93f92725124d6afa8953b7c7243aee92fcaf5) Error when a config section changes nothing the reader can see.

  [`2f3b2f2`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/2f3b2f22a30a35340549d42b09d5aaa58d9dfe61) Make the site logo a link home.

  [`8f902c6`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/8f902c66609c0bf1de6720e60552adde515209cd) Speed up the documentation build.

  [`b56b6ab`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/b56b6ab4d24ff6298b95b415b3115591e311de20) List the most recent news items on the index automatically.

  [`5b8987d`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/5b8987da0bb24fc3979fb764ccbde82b7b0e9500) Render option type examples as code blocks in the reference.

  [`b5d5274`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/b5d527471444f90df5529f15726b3d33065f9829) Scroll the menu sideways rather than wrapping it out of view.

  [`9ae2c32`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/9ae2c32a35f56f53f299e8c04f004ed3386b95e4) Highlight the current page in the menu rather than leaving it out.

  [`b8da5b0`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/b8da5b061d0f9c5d1cc01e936dc65cbdd9f70be7) Version the style and script links so stale caches are refetched.

  [`ec57431`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/ec57431aa88860b9058d971b938afb5e35a142c7) Jump straight to a section rather than gliding to it.

  [`46babed`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/46babedaed8429026efa4592d93a8d9876acad0b) Fade an end the menu carries on past rather than cutting it flat.

  [`17de501`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/17de50192b7e40d7517a4079451e93da5c39b3f3) Put the bar of a section2 header under the title rather than beside it.

  [`940f431`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/940f4311375dda3caabdc0bdd01c36bedf43d46f) Center the page title beside the logo and left align it when wrapped.

  [`050ca9e`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/050ca9e354421530ba4282d95dead90e31d6dc2a) Add expandable details to the release notes.

  [`bd8f99b`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/bd8f99be00cd4b03a1b234bf7fbaa39f406c0c44) Add release note details for v2.33 through v2.60.

  [`28a1e3d`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/28a1e3d6278992e0a347aab19248abdcddefd491) Adjust keyword styling in light mode.

  [`1911f5f`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/1911f5f1744ff4bfee528d95e37a0a8d9b77d6b3) End the release note history at upstream integration in CI.
- Document the release schedule.

  Feature releases are scheduled for the third Monday of January, April, July, and October. Patch releases are scheduled for the third Monday of any other month as required. The release notes introduction also describes the major, minor, and patch parts of a release number.

  [`9bca69a`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/9bca69a591abcee0623ff910d0db3e27008cfc25) Document the release schedule.

  [`c179f3f`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/c179f3f7d6d6b1963d9d3b98b981786ef9546022) Update release numbering in the release notes intro.
- Migrate the documentation tools from C and Perl to Python.

  The documentation is built by `doc/doc.py` instead of a C tool run by `doc/doc.pl`, and building the documentation no longer requires Perl.

  [`59bd2cd`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/59bd2cd96cfba2b02b07e81ade57d9beb5f2812e) Migrate the build and documentation code from C to Python.

  [`fa3d197`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/fa3d1979b77d6252642c64d708a951914e0a0ac2) Migrate the remaining documentation code from Perl to Python.

**Test Suite Improvements:**

- Migrate the test harness from C and Perl to Python.

  Tests are run with `test/test.py` instead of `test/test.pl`. The test containers and CI runners no longer require Perl and the harness is covered by tests.

  [`163e5ba`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/163e5ba0f9ff72ec730f0b35ddac0e5d68ece531) Migrate the test harness from C to Python.

  [`e25c662`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/e25c6621e8427e862e4ce0196d059ab651bb276d) Migrate the rest of the test harness from Perl to Python.
- Add a Fil-C job to CI.

  Fil-C is a memory-safe implementation of C that checks every pointer access. It found two out-of-bounds reads that gcc, clang, and Valgrind missed. A new workflow also builds and tests on the CI runner with gcc and clang, with and without the optional libraries.

  [`f32a5d5`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/f32a5d5543c4dc547006629bdb2dd52d3edb9205) Add a workflow to compile and test on the runner without a container.

  [`12396fb`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/12396fb353bc4aa10c3739b826189e421732adf5) Add a Fil-C job to the compile workflow.

  [`908a11a`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/908a11a841e85d4e66e4aa551380b52aabfe606f) Use the Fil-C distribution as-is now that it ships every library needed.
- Add fuzz testing for the `JSON`, `Ini`, and `Pack` readers.

  The readers are fuzzed with libFuzzer, AddressSanitizer, and UndefinedBehaviorSanitizer in CI. Fuzzing found several bugs in how invalid input is read, e.g. deeply nested JSON arrays in an info file or an object store response could overflow the memory context stack.

  Commit [`4c65010`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/4c65010e7d28ea68571732fb275d91266a97ad80)
- Allow test user names that adduser on Debian rejects. (*Contributed by Stefan Fercot. Reviewed by David Steele.*)

  The test container is built with the same user name as the host user. Debian's `adduser` rejects names that contain a dot, e.g. `first.last`, so the container build failed on Debian-based images for these users.

  Issue [#2858](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2858); Pull request [#2859](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2859); Commit [`ce19dae`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/ce19dae6a2d6ab3665374ead04c270fd45d563fb)

## Stable Releases

### v2.59.3 Release Notes {#v2593-release-notes}

*Weak Encryption Subkey Bug*

*Released October 4, 2026*

**IMPORTANT NOTE**: In prior versions it was possible for weak encryption subkeys and salts to be generated if there was an error accessing system random data. See [Weak Encryption Subkeys and Salts](/pgbackrest/news/#weak-encryption) for more information.

**Bug Fixes:**

- Fix possible checksum error loading encrypted info and manifest files. (*Fixed by j0uinim. Reviewed by David Steele.*)

  A large encrypted info or manifest file could lose its last lines when loaded, which failed the checksum. The lost lines had to fit in the final cipher block, so this is unlikely in practice.

  Issue [#2866](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2866); Pull request [#2868](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2868); Commit [`2def063`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/2def063e06c6f0b7e137309c7ed0d7631a42f6cf)
- Fix possible weak encryption subkeys/salts. (*Fixed by j0uinim. Reviewed by David Steele, Stefan Fercot, Douglas J Hunley.*)

  When OpenSSL could not get random data from the operating system the failure was ignored, and subkeys and salts were generated from memory that was not random. An error is now reported.

  Issue [#2866](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2866); Pull request [#2867](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2867); Commit [`47ed78f`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/47ed78f8d54a44466a872852939edc0273172bf1)

### v2.59.2 Release Notes

*PostgreSQL 19beta4 Support*

*Released September 27, 2026*

**Bug Fixes:**

- Fix empty block map when file is truncated to zero during backup. (*Reviewed by Douglas J Hunley. Reported by JPCOSTA78.*)

  A block incremental file larger than `repo-bundle-limit` and truncated to zero by PostgreSQL during a backup was stored as a block map with no blocks. Every later backup that referenced the file failed with "unexpected eof" until the next full backup.

  Issue [#2829](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2829); Pull request [#2842](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2842); Commit [`572d163`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/572d1631026d5f10915634592b547f3439b7a291)
- Disable read retry when filters are passed to the driver. (*Reviewed by Douglas J Hunley. Reported by megamaced.*)

  A retried read from a repository on an object store, accessed through a repository host, crashed. These reads are no longer retried by the client. The repository host still retries its read from the object store.

  Issue [#2845](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2845); Pull request [#2846](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2846); Commit [`c6eca28`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/c6eca28ff8e551bd2e76f0c91b1a70ddee60b35e)
- Fix pagination of S3 version listing. (*Reviewed by Douglas J Hunley, guruguruguru. Reported by guruguruguru.*)

  Reading a repository at a target time with `repo-target-time` failed with "unable to find child 'NextContinuationToken'" when more than a thousand versions were listed under a prefix.

  Issue [#2854](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2854); Pull request [#2855](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2855); Commit [`6f0490d`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/6f0490dd29f0c756e6766fd9be023805540815fc)
- Set the Azure version header for `SAS` authentication. (*Reviewed by Douglas J Hunley. Reported by Florian Helmberger.*)

  Batch deletes require the version header, so with `SAS` authentication every batch was rejected. The rejection looked like a single failed file, and the rest of each batch was not deleted while `expire` reported success, leaving expired backups and WAL in the repository.

  Issue [#2840](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2840); Pull request [#2848](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2848); Commit [`ea3293b`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/ea3293be75c86b5e6c9ca4158b5ebe2a3224f76f)
- Fix async `archive-push` stalling on a zero-size backup history file. (*Fixed by Andrew Kroh. Reviewed by David Steele, Douglas J Hunley.*)

  A zero-size backup history file, e.g. in a cluster copied from a storage snapshot, failed every asynchronous `archive-push` run, so no WAL was archived. `archive-push-batch-size` is now converted to a segment count using the size of the first ready WAL segment.

  Issue [#2856](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2856); Pull request [#2857](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2857); Commit [`12b1b3c`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/12b1b3cf58caee7b04ed788eee221726f5caa155)
- Fix `annotate` command on a remote encrypted repository. (*Fixed by Douglas J Hunley. Reviewed by David Steele.*)

  When the repository was encrypted and the cipher was configured only on the repository host, `annotate` failed to load `backup.info`.

  Commit [`497ad5a`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/497ad5aee0c654611d2ad4185958727e1341dd8c)

**Features:**

- PostgreSQL 19beta4/rc1 support.

  PostgreSQL 19beta4 reverted online page checksum changes, so the page checksum version is again limited to 0 and 1. It also changed the `pg_control` version and the WAL page magic number.

  Commit [`89eae83`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/89eae839d5ca79591aaea8157f437688c3b53df4)

### v2.59.1 Release Notes

*PostgreSQL 19beta3 Support*

*Released August 17, 2026*

**Bug Fixes:**

- Fix hang when the chunk buffer is smaller than the input buffer. (*Reviewed by Andrew Pogrebnoi, Douglas J Hunley. Reported by crajac66.*)

  A multi-part upload to S3, GCS, or Azure could loop forever when the chunk buffer was smaller than the input buffer, e.g. after the chunk size grew during the upload. The chunk buffer is now always large enough to hold the input buffer.

  Issue [#2818](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2818); Pull request [#2819](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2819); Commit [`a53e470`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/a53e470eca258c2db088af55be307403c8f43b7a)
- Compare a new backup label only against its own full backup set. (*Reviewed by Douglas J Hunley. Reported by Anton Glushakov.*)

  Some object stores keep listing a prefix after everything under it has been deleted, e.g. MinIO with object locking or SeaweedFS, so a full backup that was removed could still appear in the repository. Every diff and incr backup after it then failed with "new backup label ... is not later than latest backup label ...". A new label is now compared only against the backups in its own full backup set.

  Issue [#2792](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2792); Pull request [#2824](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2824); Commit [`d01f77e`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/d01f77e8237f5d008bb7782c18ced12cd6d1971a)
- Fix source archives generated by GitHub missing files required to build. (*Reported by aardvarkzed.*)

  The source archives GitHub generates for a release were missing the code generation tooling and the files it reads, so they could not be built. Only the distribution tarball is trimmed now.

  Issue [#2821](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2821); Commit [`163670a`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/163670a4eeede6d2d170b5d1f55c5ba0b66ba564)

**Features:**

- PostgreSQL 19beta3 support. (*Contributed by Lardière Sébastien. Reviewed by David Steele.*)

  PostgreSQL 19beta3 changed the WAL page magic number.

  Commit [`5cbc256`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/5cbc256bf37a44fa478d73a12a2e5b2d84fa6337)

**Documentation Improvements:**

- Document exception for dots in S3 bucket names when `repo-s3-uri-style=path`.

  With `repo-s3-uri-style=path` the bucket is part of the path instead of the host name, so dots in the bucket name do not cause a certificate host name mismatch.

  Commit [`bbdc4b9`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/bbdc4b9217a0e66f6a01c05f4edc04282cffb3fc)
- Remove explicit `hot_standby` configuration from user guide.

  `hot_standby` has been enabled by default since PostgreSQL 10. The quickstart now notes that a standby does not accept read-only connections without it.

  Commit [`cda454d`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/cda454d4bac0c0412230a0b41513659a7d0d4205)

### v2.59.0 Release Notes

*PostgreSQL 19beta2 Support*

*Released July 20, 2026*

**NOTE TO PACKAGERS**: A new distribution tarball is available which simplifies the build process by providing pregenerated HTML documentation, man page, and code. The tarball is attached to each release as an asset and is named `pgbackrest-{version}.tar.gz`. See the `README.md` in the tarball for details. Please use the new distribution tarball to avoid the additional build tooling required to generate code in future releases. See [New Distribution Tarball](/pgbackrest/news/#distribution-tarball) for more information.<br/> <br/> **NOTE TO PACKAGERS**: There is a new optional dependency on `libsystemd`.<br/> <br/> **IMPORTANT NOTE**: Starting with this release, only the `restore` command may be run as root by default. Use `allow-root` to run other commands as root (though this is not recommended).

**Bug Fixes:**

- Fix `expire` removing a backup in progress on another host. (*Reviewed by Douglas J Hunley. Reported by Dmitrii.*)

  A backup is not added to `backup.info` until it completes, so when `expire` ran on one host while a backup was running on another, e.g. `expire` on a standby while the primary is backed up, the backup in progress was removed. Expiration no longer removes the latest backup when it is in progress or resumable.

  Issue [#2622](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2622); Pull request [#2797](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2797); Commit [`aa0e215`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/aa0e215019f50f92a8f0c97f907affc1d3bf86c4)
- Fix `archive-push-queue-max` not enforced when a WAL file errors. (*Reviewed by Stefan Fercot. Reported by vut12.*)

  In asynchronous mode the queue was measured against the WAL yet to be pushed. When a WAL file errored repeatedly the WAL files after it were pushed and no longer counted, although they stayed in `pg_wal`, so the queue limit was never reached and `pg_wal` could fill the disk. The queue is now measured against all the WAL PostgreSQL is waiting to archive, as in synchronous mode.

  Issue [#2629](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2629); Pull request [#2799](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2799); Commit [`7fe3636`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/7fe363658639bbc2f70e53496fa6da4acb9e481e)
- Fix async `archive-get` failure during recovery across a timeline switch. (*Reviewed by Douglas J Hunley. Reported by Jimmy Yih.*)

  Two async processes could race for the archive lock, and the one that lost exited before fetching the segment it was started for. The `archive-get` that started it then waited for `archive-timeout` and returned an error for a segment that exists, which could end recovery early, especially across a timeline switch. The segment is now fetched directly when the async process that should fetch it is not running.

  Issue [#2423](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2423); Pull request [#2806](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2806); Commit [`baed942`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/baed942af0d61fcf3c2cb1f7d1bf1d6fe5bb6df9)
- Fix potential buffer overrun in error module. (*Fixed by Christophe Pettus. Reviewed by David Steele.*)

  A stack trace too long for its buffer was not terminated and could be read past the end of the buffer, and an error message too long for its buffer could write past the end of it when a system error was appended. Both require an unusually long message.

  [`7fd8bc8`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/7fd8bc89a15cb039111aa85fd37f9dc68f7c4d6d) Fix potential buffer overrun in error module.

  [`5ca98ab`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/5ca98abc91f823ef97ff6a1e77d65727d1259218) Use the full buffer when formatting error messages.

  [`3a71dc8`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/3a71dc839a89b83f592c1d5813c068bcded1b6bc) Prevent an out-of-bounds error buffer write on an oversized message.
- Fix use-after-free of parallel protocol client. (*Reviewed by Douglas J Hunley. Reported by Georgy Shelkovy.*)

  A client that ran out of work was freed but left in the client list and freed again on a later call. With debug or trace logging this read freed memory and could crash, e.g. a parallel backup with `log-level-file=trace`.

  Issue [#2754](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2754); Pull request [#2804](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2804); Commit [`31432ed`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/31432ed3977302b5e183f2b592d85afb521b4f55)
- Fix heap overflow when a pg option is set without pg1. (*Reviewed by Douglas J Hunley. Reported by Naveen.*)

  When `pg` options were set for other indexes without `pg1-path`, e.g. `pg2-path` and `pg3-path`, the index map was overwritten. This corrupted the heap on musl, e.g. Alpine, and caused a segmentation fault. The highest numbered `pg-path` was also dropped.

  Issue [#1768](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/1768); Pull request [#2796](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2796); Commit [`2d767de`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/2d767de3c59cf5570639e75755dc73c626fb99e1)
- Fix crash when a signal arrives during exit on musl 1.2.6.

  musl 1.2.6, e.g. on Alpine 3.24, crashes when `exit()` is called while an exit is already in progress. This happened when a signal arrived during exit, e.g. when the `server` command terminated a child process that was already exiting. A signal that arrives during exit is now ignored.

  Commit [`e166a33`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/e166a33f48a00192c09b0f1cea3465243c03d1f1)

**Features:**

- PostgreSQL 19beta1/beta2 support. (*Reviewed by Stefan Fercot.*)

  Adds support for PostgreSQL 19beta1/beta2. PostgreSQL 19 can enable and disable page checksums online, and page checksums are not validated while either is in progress.

  [`bc6d399`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/bc6d399a6a160f5fa41dd3a98ae046dff66e56a8) PostgreSQL 19beta1 support.; Pull request [#2779](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2779)

  [`2777395`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/2777395dacc35aec51572d3e75c621725e2f9d9c) PostgreSQL 19beta2 support.
- Add `archive-expire-before` option to clean up WAL archive. (*Contributed by Stefan Fercot. Reviewed by David Steele.*)

  The `expire` option `archive-expire-before` removes WAL before a given segment from the archive but never WAL that a retained backup requires. It can clean up the archive from the restartpoint of a standby with `archive_cleanup_command` instead of `pg_archivecleanup`, which does not know about retention.

  [`6cd692b`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/6cd692b1b321faf544ee3a9b1f503fd48007d7c7) Extract WAL expiration into expireArchiveId().

  [`4945139`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/4945139392c21f197f2e307abc3c89b5548cef8d) Add archive-expire-before option to clean up WAL archive.; Pull request [#2801](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2801)
- Add batch delete for Azure storage. (*Reviewed by Douglas J Hunley, Crispy.*)

  Files are removed with the Azure Blob Batch API, up to 256 per request, instead of one request per file. This speeds up `expire` and other commands that remove files from the repository.

  Pull request [#2786](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2786); Commit [`ffa325b`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/ffa325b8a44c1e9e44dc038bbcc8aece6f312a90)
- Add support for S3 Outposts. (*Contributed by Shiva Kumar Ambigi. Reviewed by David Steele, Roberto Mello.*)

  Set `repo-s3-service=s3-outposts` when using an S3 Outposts endpoint. The option sets the service name used for request signing and defaults to `s3`.

  Pull request [#2762](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2762); Commit [`6c51800`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/6c518001c24ad2405eaf750d358f69703322a221)
- Add S3 process authentication. (*Reviewed by Andrew Charlton. Suggested by Andrew Charlton.*)

  Set `repo-s3-key-type=process` and set `repo-s3-process-cmd` to a command that outputs temporary credentials in the JSON format of the AWS process credential provider. The credentials are refreshed before they expire.

  Pull request [#2778](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2778); Commit [`b5a578a`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/b5a578a5c2c88d53fca0ee8cfbfb8ee662ce7e48)
- Provide a distribution tarball with generated code and documentation.

  The tarball includes the generated code and the rendered man page and HTML documentation, so it builds and installs without the code generation and documentation tooling, including `libyaml`.

  Commit [`8fcfef5`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/8fcfef5d8732055f9bc8e50224d5b7a988512e61)

**Improvements:**

- Reconnect SFTP storage after the server drops an idle connection. (*Reviewed by Stefan Fercot. Suggested by mustafa0x, tisserat.*)

  SFTP servers and firewalls may drop a connection that has been idle for a while, e.g. the repository read connection during the copy phase of a backup, and the next use failed with a socket error. A dropped connection is now reopened and the operation is retried once.

  [`d72bea9`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/d72bea9fe7c3975fc6832a51b0b0c1c041c467d2) Fetch the libssh2 session from storage in SFTP read/write objects.

  [`a31febf`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/a31febfbd9ba639589fe57e4083ae9358b1306a4) Reconnect SFTP storage after the server drops an idle connection.; Issue [#2739](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2739); Issue [#2772](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2772); Pull request [#2793](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2793)
- Add user/group caching for faster manifest build. (*Contributed by Gunnar Lindholm. Reviewed by David Steele.*)

  When user and group names are looked up through a remote name service, e.g. sssd or LDAP, each lookup is a round trip, which dominated the manifest build for clusters with millions of files. User and group names are now cached.

  Pull request [#2773](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2773); Commit [`1e6d23f`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/1e6d23fea72ac9c599f8b62a2e0a3de02d0d08d0)
- Add per-repo backup progress to `info` command output. (*Contributed by Will Morland. Reviewed by David Steele, Stefan Fercot.*)

  When backups run on more than one repository at once, the `info` command now reports progress for each repository as well as overall progress. The JSON output has a `repo` array for each backup lock.

  Issue [#2736](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2736); Pull request [#2740](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2740); Commit [`77312b3`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/77312b33ba913132b33401d862c2e354f323c786)
- Add `backup.info` checks to `verify` command. (*Contributed by Denis Garsh. Reviewed by David Steele, Douglas J Hunley.*)

  The `verify` command now warns when a backup in the repository is not in `backup.info` and when a backup in `backup.info` is missing from the repository. A missing backup is reported as having a missing manifest.

  Issue [#2723](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2723); Pull request [#2752](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2752); Commit [`7f82fb6`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/7f82fb6635c1e3e9dd285d53076e9aee3fbf1ca2)
- Allow the S3 STS endpoint to be configured. (*Contributed by Simon Gratton. Reviewed by David Steele.*)

  Set `repo-s3-sts-host` to use a regional STS endpoint with `repo-s3-key-type=web-id`, e.g. `sts.eu-central-2.amazonaws.com`. AWS opt-in regions do not accept credentials from the global endpoint, which is still the default.

  Issue [#2791](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2791); Pull request [#2798](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2798); Commit [`11f5582`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/11f5582dfe696187cab1f15d08f649a5117730b3)
- Add `archive-push-batch-size` to limit WAL pushed per asynchronous run. (*Reviewed by Stefan Fercot.*)

  In asynchronous mode `archive-push-queue-max` is only checked at the start of a run, so a run working through a large backlog could let the queue grow well past the limit. `archive-push-batch-size` (default 16GiB) limits the WAL pushed per run, so the queue is checked again sooner.

  Pull request [#2803](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2803); Commit [`34a035f`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/34a035f4d62f06822c1f60f72a605762cc1ad79c)
- Exit async `archive-push` on first error. (*Reviewed by Stefan Fercot. Suggested by Lardière Sébastien.*)

  When the repository was unavailable the async process kept trying every ready WAL segment, so a run could take long enough for `pg_wal` to fill before `archive-push-queue-max` was checked again. The run now exits on the first error, after the jobs in progress finish.

  Issue [#1848](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/1848); Pull request [#2802](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2802); Commit [`a0c1435`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/a0c14359392e283d88f72e7f9f815d9e78ddfb10)
- Harden HTTP chunked response parsing. (*Contributed by Shubham. Reviewed by David Steele.*)

  Chunk extensions and trailers are now accepted, `transfer-encoding` is compared without regard to case, and a response with both `transfer-encoding` and `content-length` is rejected.

  Pull request [#2785](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2785); Commit [`34bba82`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/34bba828c6ba97579d65c2199c02d94bf31257a5)
- Error when running as root unless `allow-root` is enabled. (*Reviewed by Douglas J Hunley.*)

  Running a command other than `restore` as root could create files in the repository, e.g. `backup.info`, that the PostgreSQL user cannot access. `allow-root` is disabled by default for every command except `restore`, and the check also applies on remote hosts.

  Issue [#1693](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/1693); Pull request [#2812](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2812); Commit [`0daf53d`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/0daf53da03fb179b5771b6aaa559f9e0cc5842bb)
- Fewer binary searches when building manifest. (*Contributed by Gunnar Lindholm. Reviewed by David Steele.*)

  The manifest build for a diff or incr backup searched the prior manifest three times for each file. It now searches once, which saves millions of searches for clusters with millions of files.

  Pull request [#2774](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2774); Commit [`f1ef629`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/f1ef62956adabf305a7e959477ea1bb668fd8d65)
- Improve seek performance during block incremental delta restore. (*Reviewed by David Christensen, Douglas J Hunley.*)

  A seek and a flush of the write buffer were done for every block written during a block incremental delta restore, and blocks are often 8KiB. The file position is now tracked and the seek and flush are only done when needed.

  Pull request [#2732](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2732); Commit [`b908d5e`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/b908d5e2c5b37ef968f19e2c40a1001e19e5b87f)
- Bundle backup files in order rather than scanning for a better fit. (*Reviewed by Stefan Fercot.*)

  A file that would push a bundle over `repo-bundle-size` was skipped for a smaller file later in the queue. Files are now added in order and a bundle is closed once it reaches `repo-bundle-size`, so a bundle may exceed it by up to `repo-bundle-limit`.

  Pull request [#2794](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2794); Commit [`253dbc3`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/253dbc3f03e5581c98bb97b252dc77b8defd3693)
- Report the underlying error when a query fails to complete. (*Reviewed by Andrew Pogrebnoi. Suggested by Marco Fontana.*)

  When the connection was lost after the first result of a query was delivered, e.g. under load, the error was the generic "NULL result required to complete request". The error from PostgreSQL is now reported, with hints about a lost connection and about aggressive `idle_session_timeout` and `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout` settings.

  Issue [#2789](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2789); Pull request [#2790](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2790); Commit [`23e966c`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/23e966cd8f17a8cd446b04901d0ae66ebf4118ee)
- Report the actual error when an S3 credential request fails. (*Reviewed by Douglas J Hunley. Suggested by Mitchell Grice.*)

  An error from AWS for a `web-id` or `pod-id` credential request was reported as a failure to parse the response. The error returned by AWS is now reported, and the tokens are redacted when the request is logged.

  Issue [#1997](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/1997); Pull request [#2808](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2808); Commit [`b0c57c2`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/b0c57c280d727e743b72a6d86e2c3e413307e44b)
- Report `archive-push` spool path errors to the PostgreSQL log. (*Reviewed by Douglas J Hunley. Suggested by Don Seiler.*)

  When the async spool path could not be created, e.g. permission was denied, the error could not be written to the spool and `archive-push` failed with a generic timeout that pointed at the async log. The spool path is now created before the async process starts, so the error appears in the PostgreSQL log.

  Issue [#1408](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/1408); Pull request [#2810](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2810); Commit [`7b0159c`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/7b0159c7dfd69763b63b1385fd564c18a4a5c1bc)
- Hint that pgBackRest may be out of date when a version is unsupported.

  When pgBackRest does not recognize a PostgreSQL version, e.g. an older pgBackRest on the restore host reading a backup of a newer cluster, the error now hints that pgBackRest may need to be updated on all hosts. The version is reported in user-facing form, e.g. 13 rather than 130000.

  Commit [`d4f697e`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/d4f697ebce705f9439798f970103fd38f829dfff)
- Add systemd notify integration. (*Contributed by Andrew Jackson. Reviewed by David Steele.*)

  When built with `libsystemd`, the `server` command notifies systemd when it is ready to accept connections and when it is stopping, as PostgreSQL and PgBouncer do.

  Issue [#2757](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2757); Pull request [#2760](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2760); Commit [`742fff1`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/742fff174a3f5baaed932afa284334ca2aa1be82)
- Suppress `unused parameter` errors in meson compiler probes. (*Contributed by Jörg Plate. Reviewed by David Steele.*)

  The meson compiler probes declared `main()` parameters they did not use, which failed the probes when unused parameter warnings were errors.

  Issue [#2724](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2724); Commit [`d4465bd`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/d4465bd6f78d6d58d18ebedbe8f63b55d0ae2508)
- C11 is now the minimum C standard. (*Reviewed by Mohammad Ali Nazir Kosar.*)

  Pull request [#2738](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2738); Commit [`3a6c671`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/3a6c67183cd9c6a12c24f0d978ef5e501b7c3fcf)

**Documentation Improvements:**

- Make `stanza` option internal for the `repo-*` commands. (*Reviewed by Stefan Fercot.*)

  The `stanza` option only has an effect on the `repo-*` commands with undocumented storage path constants, so the documentation implied it did something it does not. The option is no longer documented for these commands and works as before.

  Issue [#2735](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2735); Commit [`3e9eabd`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/3e9eabd8a5551cdaeb6bc78a52f000eaf239defb)
- Document that info reads encryption settings from global section. (*Reviewed by Stefan Fercot. Suggested by Ron Johnson.*)

  When run without `stanza` the `info` command reads encryption settings only from the global section, so encryption settings configured for a stanza require `stanza`.

  Issue [#1407](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/1407); Pull request [#2805](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2805); Commit [`2137bda`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/2137bda1a5a1ad890d0137feffe8491102041af3)
- Document that `expire-auto` uses the `backup` command configuration. (*Reviewed by Stefan Fercot. Suggested by Lardière Sébastien.*)

  Automatic expiration after a backup uses the configuration of the `backup` command, so options set only for the `expire` command are not applied. Disable `expire-auto` and run `expire` separately when it needs its own configuration.

  Issue [#1874](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/1874); Pull request [#2807](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2807); Commit [`cf847f9`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/cf847f94cd7ab6517ec1abb30075fb53dc75d5f8)
- Document adjusting the logrotate `su` directive for a dedicated user. (*Reviewed by Douglas J Hunley. Suggested by lkanbus.*)

  Packages may install a logrotate configuration that rotates the logs as the `postgres` user. When a dedicated user owns the repository and logs, the `su` directive must be changed to that user or rotation fails with a permission error.

  Issue [#2737](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2737); Pull request [#2809](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2809); Commit [`bde2c50`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/bde2c509c923fbe2844c10b0f95c55973378d2ae)
- Document that end-of-line comments are not supported in config files. (*Reviewed by Douglas J Hunley. Suggested by Alex Richman.*)

  End-of-line comments have never been supported, although they may appear to work for some options. Only comments on a line of their own are supported.

  Issue [#1633](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/1633); Pull request [#2811](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2811); Commit [`8571799`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/85717992cadbfdabc571fb23914b62b08c7f6f44)

**Test Suite Improvements:**

- Fix Alpine group conflicts in CI containers. (*Contributed by Artur Zakirov. Reviewed by David Steele.*)

  Group entries with members are now handled when the test group is renamed, and the Alpine group is only created when it does not already exist.

  Pull request [#2784](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2784); Commit [`760dd8d`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/760dd8db69e7c6e5cbb9c5b7e2067b6813cf4497)

### v2.58.0 Release Notes

*Object Storage Improvements*

*Released January 19, 2026*

**IMPORTANT NOTE**: The minimum values for the `repo-storage-upload-chunk-size` option have increased. They now represent the minimum allowed by the vendors.

**Bug Fixes:**

- Fix deadlock due to logging in signal handler. (*Fixed by Maxim Michkov. Reviewed by David Steele.*)

  A signal that arrived while a lock was held by `gmtime_r()`, e.g. the SIGTERM sent by `stop` with `--force`, could deadlock when the signal handler logged. The signal handler now logs with only async-signal-safe functions.

  Issue [#2710](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2710); Pull request [#2715](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2715); Commit [`e13c462`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/e13c462908c6ee25f8795b5d1373981549d4d5cf)

**Features:**

- HTTP support for S3, GCS, and Azure. (*Contributed by Will Morland. Reviewed by David Steele.*)

  An object store endpoint can now begin with `http://` to connect without TLS. HTTPS is still used when the endpoint does not specify a protocol.

  Issue [#2340](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2340); Pull request [#2703](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2703); Commit [`2398dd4`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/2398dd4d432d312b3641e9fbbcbe0487ad43112d)
- Allow expiration of oldest full backup regardless of current retention. (*Contributed by Stefan Fercot. Reviewed by David Steele. Suggested by Ron Johnson.*)

  The `expire` option `--oldest` expires the oldest full backup and the backups that depend on it regardless of the retention settings, e.g. when the repository is running out of space. WAL before the oldest remaining full backup is expired as well.

  Issue [#2666](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2666); Pull request [#2709](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2709); Commit [`bf2b276`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/bf2b276dc011464b13039630870934fd42ca473c)
- Support for Azure managed identities. (*Contributed by Moiz Ibrar, Matthew Mols. Reviewed by David Steele.*)

  Set `repo-azure-key-type=auto` to fetch credentials automatically using managed identities instead of configuring them. The credentials are refreshed before they expire.

  Issue [#2023](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2023); Pull request [#2712](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2712); Commit [`38f14d7`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/38f14d73b6a62d06e1ead7942e49a2bb2ae90dab)
- ***Experimental*** support for S3 EKS pod identity. (*Contributed by Pierre BOUTELOUP. Reviewed by David Steele.*)

  Set `repo-s3-key-type=pod-id` to fetch credentials automatically using EKS pod identity instead of configuring them. The credentials are refreshed before they expire.

  Issue [#2718](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2718); Pull request [#2719](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2719); Commit [`79544f6`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/79544f64a3ce198f4685f923739def997fcafbce)
- Allow configuration of TLS cipher suites. (*Contributed by Gunnar "Nick" Bluth. Reviewed by David Steele.*)

  Set `tls-cipher-12` for TLS 1.2 and `tls-cipher-13` for TLS 1.3, e.g. for compliance. The cipher suites apply to every connection a process makes.

  [`fbc607a`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/fbc607a48b1fb4ae65c9f2691c44453f02aef2b8) Allow configuration of TLS cipher suites.; Issue [#2697](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2697); Pull request [#2700](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2700)

  [`91ad655`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/91ad65537f55aa3825b6c7b626a9e1b7cbb1ad4c) Fix flapping common/io-tls unit test added in fbc607a4.
- Allow process priority to be set. (*Reviewed by Douglas J Hunley.*)

  `priority` sets the scheduling priority of pgBackRest processes from -20 to 19, as with `nice`. A higher value makes pgBackRest less likely to interfere with PostgreSQL.

  Pull request [#2693](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2693); Commit [`0fc7e96`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/0fc7e96f5addb01e7f6f3585e37d209cc17dd956)

**Improvements:**

- Allow dots in S3 bucket names when using path-style URIs. (*Contributed by Joakim Hindersson. Reviewed by David Steele.*)

  Dots in a bucket name only cause a certificate host name mismatch with host-style URIs, so they are now allowed with `repo-s3-uri-style=path`, which most self-hosted S3 services use.

  Pull request [#2713](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2713); Commit [`3fdb4a1`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/3fdb4a1ea00b442aca5fc714818d293d57490386)
- Require TLS >= 1.2 unless verification is disabled. (*Reviewed by Douglas J Hunley, Gunnar "Nick" Bluth.*)

  TLS 1.0 and 1.1 have been deprecated since 2021. Verification is always enabled for connections between pgBackRest hosts. When verification is disabled for an object store with `repo-storage-verify-tls`, e.g. for a self-signed certificate, every protocol version is allowed.

  Pull request [#2720](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2720); Commit [`5b4b20a`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/5b4b20a8d7d17c2bffe106595310853e3f3a9604)
- Dynamically size S3/GCS/Azure chunks for large uploads. (*Reviewed by Douglas J Hunley. Suggested by Timothée Peignier.*)

  The number of chunks in an upload is limited, so with the default `repo-storage-upload-chunk-size` the largest file that could be uploaded was 50GiB to S3, 40GiB to GCS, and 200GiB to Azure. The chunk size now grows during an upload, so files up to 5TiB can be uploaded to S3 and GCS and up to 45TiB to Azure.

  Issue [#2679](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2679); Pull request [#2701](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2701); Commit [`86554fa`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/86554faa707292855c2776a0c70b5e212b1e3945)
- Optimize S3/GCS/Azure chunk size for small files. (*Reviewed by Douglas J Hunley.*)

  The whole chunk buffer was allocated when a file was opened, although many files are smaller than a chunk. The buffer now grows as data arrives, which saves memory when small files are uploaded.

  Pull request [#2705](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2705); Commit [`28bfa20`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/28bfa20ba3b860f72ba7ab6c018e37ada99e2109)
- Remove support for PostgreSQL `9.5`. (*Reviewed by Douglas J Hunley.*)

  PostgreSQL 9.5 is no longer supported, per the policy of supporting five end-of-life versions of PostgreSQL.

  Pull request [#2692](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2692); Commit [`5171e8b`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/5171e8bde31b9711c9c5b4ccc5170b61c5235548)
- Improve logging of default for options with an unresolved dependency. (*Reviewed by Stefan Fercot.*)

  An option set in the configuration file was logged at the start of a command when its dependency was not resolved, although its default value was used. The option is no longer logged in this case.

  Issue [#2707](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2707); Pull request [#2708](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2708); Commit [`38ac638`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/38ac6387cbbfc6f78a686de657e5a4de4ed59295)

**Documentation Improvements:**

- Remove explicit `max_wal_senders`/`wal_level` configuration from user guide. (*Suggested by Jamie Nguyen.*)

  `max_wal_senders` and `wal_level` have defaulted to the values the user guide requires since PostgreSQL 10.

  Issue [#2699](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2699); Commit [`bce97d3`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/bce97d3ea80675b8eab3dbf692e8bc8f077f30c7)
- Clarify that bundling is useful for filesystems with large block sizes. (*Suggested by Ron Johnson.*)

  Bundling saves space on filesystems with large block sizes, where each small file takes a whole block.

  Issue [#2706](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2706); Commit [`a7e5f28`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/a7e5f28511c27f2504a873db26803b26a84df8f1)

### v2.57.0 Release Notes

*Suppress Repository Symlinks*

*Released October 18, 2025*

**Bug Fixes:**

- Unnest HTTP/TLS/socket timeouts. (*Reviewed by David Christensen.*)

  A failed socket connection was also retried by the TLS and HTTP layers, so with the default `io-timeout` of 60 seconds a connection took nine minutes to time out. Each layer now retries only its own failures.

  Issue [#2654](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2654); Pull request [#2662](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2662); Commit [`9c7f064`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/9c7f0648648e651d82918e5dbfc3d511abee3298)
- Fix possible segfault in page checksum error message. (*Fixed by Zsolt Parragi. Reviewed by David Steele.*)

  The error message for invalid page checksums used a string that was out of scope, which could log garbage or crash. This was most likely with pg_tde, since every encrypted file fails the page checksum.

  Pull request [#2675](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2675); Commit [`adc5e5b`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/adc5e5b23b9b544745724a7eaa0461b88b691e36)
- Fix possible segfault in backup resume messages.

  The message that reports why a backup cannot be resumed used a string that was out of scope, which could log garbage or crash.

  Commit [`c1c40a4`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/c1c40a4a851efe98be83aca2044c02fd1c7fb2ae)

**Features:**

- Add `repo-symlink` option to suppress creation of repository symlinks. (*Reviewed by Douglas J Hunley. Suggested by Ron Johnson.*)

  Some filesystems that are otherwise Posix-compatible do not support symlinks. Set `repo-symlink=n` to keep the `latest` and tablespace symlinks from being created in the repository.

  Issue [#2673](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2673); Pull request [#2674](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2674); Commit [`bfd9790`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/bfd97907311a25ed239798691cff52cf73acdb83)

**Improvements:**

- Add HTTP retries for 408 and 429 errors. (*Reviewed by David Christensen.*)

  HTTP 408 (Request Timeout) and 429 (Too Many Requests) errors are now retried. 429 errors are common on Cloudflare R2.

  Pull request [#2661](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2661); Commit [`8733900`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/87339004e59c03d2a03a6ceb8426202346849c37)

### v2.56.0 Release Notes

*Progress Info Improvements*

*Released July 21, 2025*

**Bug Fixes:**

- Fix issue with adhoc expiration when no backups in a repository. (*Reviewed by Stefan Fercot. Reported by Anup Gupta.*)

  Adhoc expiration failed with "cannot get index 0 from list with 0 value(s)" when a repository had no backups.

  Issue [#2645](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2645); Pull request [#2646](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2646); Commit [`638815b`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/638815b4cde1ba1322516e256742c51676af66f0)

**Features:**

- Add restore progress to `info` command output. (*Contributed by Denis Garsh, Maxim Michkov. Reviewed by David Steele.*)

  The `restore` command now records its progress, which the `info` command reports alongside backup progress. A restore is also no longer terminated by the `stop` command.

  Issue [#2546](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2546); Pull request [#2652](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2652); Commit [`8cdd9ce`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/8cdd9ce1c4ab6cca508932a41a3013374d7547ef)
- Add progress-only detail level for `info` command output. (*Contributed by Denis Garsh. Reviewed by David Steele, Stefan Fercot.*)

  Set `--detail-level=progress` to report only progress, which is faster because the repository is not read. The default is `full`.

  Pull request [#2550](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2550); Commit [`2706168`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/2706168f609af544b0453d384c7fb620626fc205)

**Improvements:**

- Retry failed reads on object stores. (*Reviewed by David Christensen.*)

  A read from an object store that failed after it started was fatal, which could restart an expensive job, e.g. during restore. A failed read is now retried from where it left off.

  Pull request [#2642](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2642); Commit [`5e14692`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/5e14692588b9fd8a566c9a1795c866ba2e83eebd)
- Fix defaults in command-line help. (*Reviewed by David Christensen, Chris Bandy.*)

  When an option has more than one default, e.g. `repo-storage-upload-chunk-size` with an S3 and an Azure repository, the option list shows `<multi>` and the help for the option lists each default. Defaults are not shown for options that are not valid in the current configuration.

  Pull request [#2625](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2625); Commit [`8737cad`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/8737cad566974c37529595715ecdc06571420752)

**Documentation Improvements:**

- Describe discrete option values in a list where appropriate. (*Contributed by Anton Kurochkin. Reviewed by David Steele.*)

  The values of options such as `output` for the `verify` and `version` commands are now described as a list, as for other options.

  Pull request [#2638](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2638); Commit [`2e76e9f`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/2e76e9fc699d22286f6bf05fddc271d8fa22523e)
- Fix "less than" in help output for `archive-mode` option. (*Contributed by Anton Kurochkin. Reviewed by David Steele.*)

  Pull request [#2633](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2633); Commit [`cb67322`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/cb673225e90604a8865ce30c188d15661f945e5e)

### v2.55.1 Release Notes

*Bug Fixes*

*Released May 5, 2025*

**Bug Fixes:**

- Revert "calculate content-md5 on S3 only when required". (*Reviewed by David Christensen. Reported by Frank Brendel.*)

  S3 requires either `content-md5` or an `x-amz-content-*` checksum for uploads when object lock is enabled, and Scality did not accept the alternate checksums, so uploads failed. `content-md5` is calculated for all requests with content again.

  Issue [#2613](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2613); Pull request [#2615](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2615); Commit [`8bbb982`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/8bbb98243566bfa052f6aacfd1bd3a6acd770e40)
- Fix lower bounds checking for option keys. (*Reviewed by David Christensen, Wolfgang Walther. Reported by Wolfgang Walther.*)

  An indexed option with a key of 0, e.g. `pg0-path`, caused a segfault. It is now an error.

  Issue [#2610](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2610); Pull request [#2611](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2611); Commit [`5e01098`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/5e01098617a3803067b153be2d41e023cd4d95e4)

### v2.55.0 Release Notes

*Verification Improvements and PostgreSQL 18 Support*

*Released April 21, 2025*

**Bug Fixes:**

- Fix block incremental restore issue on non-default repository. (*Reviewed by David Christensen. Tested by Aleksander Łukasz. Reported by Aleksander Łukasz.*)

  When the backup being restored used block incremental and was not in the lowest numbered repository, the restore read part of the block incremental data from the lowest numbered repository. Setting `repo` worked around the problem.

  Issue [#2577](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2577); Pull request [#2579](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2579); Commit [`5d2c67c`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/5d2c67c3c6975ea3ed08c7425f6e410ae378913f)
- Do not set `recovery_target_timeline=current` for PostgreSQL < 12. (*Reviewed by Stefan Fercot.*)

  Before PostgreSQL 12, explicitly setting `recovery_target_timeline` to `current` behaves as `latest`, although `current` is the default. The setting is now left out of `recovery.conf` when it is set to `current` for these versions.

  Pull request [#2533](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2533); Commit [`6fbb28f`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/6fbb28fa2d615576be78376c7f539921f6b8bc6a)
- Fix expire archive range logging. (*Reviewed by Stefan Fercot. Reported by Aleš Zelený.*)

  Only the last archive path removed was logged as the start of the range, so the log suggested that far fewer files were expired than were.

  Issue [#2543](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2543); Pull request [#2554](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2554); Commit [`47f050f`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/47f050f8fd816e57326d881f4253d8a13c0f8da6)
- Fix error reporting for queries with no results. (*Reviewed by Stefan Fercot. Reported by Susantha Bathige.*)

  An error from a query that expected no results was reported as an unexpected result instead of as the error.

  Pull request [#2539](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2539); Commit [`d5cefb7`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/d5cefb7290c34c64a2b9aa89ff04a718df4ec18b)

**Features:**

- Verify recovery target timeline. (*Reviewed by Stefan Fercot.*)

  A backup that cannot follow the target timeline, e.g. one made after a standby was promoted and the new timeline forked off, failed only when recovery started, with a PostgreSQL error that is hard to interpret. The timeline is now checked against the history files before the restore starts.

  Pull request [#2534](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2534); Commit [`922e9f0`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/922e9f0775c39b64e009251a6bec8ef9b83c190f)
- Allow verification of a specified backup. (*Contributed by Maxim Michkov. Reviewed by David Steele.*)

  The `verify` option `--set` verifies only the specified backup and the WAL required to make it consistent.

  Issue [#2419](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2419); Pull request [#2544](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2544); Commit [`e4def88`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/e4def8845eb5c3fe9fb8cc44d9f64766cfb348c2)
- Add support for S3/GCS requester pays. (*Contributed by Timothée Peignier. Reviewed by David Steele.*)

  Set `repo-s3-requester-pays` or `repo-gcs-user-project` to bill the requester instead of the bucket owner, e.g. so a hosted service can give customers access to their backups.

  [`4db0263`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/4db0263fd63274a8168eb4de17b320d2f86251a2) Add support for S3 requester pays.; Pull request [#2560](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2560)

  [`814bf48`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/814bf487c3955e877d226faaa22324d3c79863b2) Add support for GCS requester pays.; Pull request [#2557](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2557)
- PostgreSQL 18 support. (*Reviewed by Stefan Fercot.*)

  This release was made before PostgreSQL 18 had a beta, so support was initially experimental. Nothing that affects pgBackRest changed before PostgreSQL 18.0.

  Pull request [#2603](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2603); Commit [`565d2e0`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/565d2e0c47cd21df890a40b3a94a8c865bed76d9)
- Allow connections to PostgreSQL on abstract domain sockets. (*Tested by Chris Bandy. Suggested by Chris Bandy.*)

  `pg-socket-path` no longer has to be an absolute path, so it can name an abstract domain socket. libpq reports a path that is not valid.

  Issue [#2529](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2529); Pull request [#2535](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2535); Commit [`68f22ae`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/68f22aea6665b9dcbe76dbfaa731f37187e1ce20)
- Add numeric output to `version` command. (*Contributed by Stefan Fercot. Reviewed by David Steele.*)

  Set `--output=num` to output the version as a number, e.g. 2055000, which is easier to compare.

  Issue [#2410](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2410); Pull request [#2526](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2526); Commit [`750a051`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/750a051b7aeccc3dd7ae9c07af2c73cb25057de9)

**Improvements:**

- Allow backup command to operate on remote repositories. (*Reviewed by Stefan Fercot.*)

  The `backup` command can now run on a host where the repository is remote, which allows more flexibility in where backups are run.

  [`4a94b6b`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/4a94b6bef9108ab775c0070571a1fb7dd8883eeb) Refactor protocol helper.

  [`931435c`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/931435c0177ded2189903385d520d5c8819c4f5e) Allow backup command to operate on remote repositories.; Pull request [#2512](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2512)
- Use lz4 for protocol compression. (*Reviewed by Stefan Fercot.*)

  lz4 compresses much faster than gz and gives similar ratios at the low levels used for protocol compression. Setting `compress-level-network=0` no longer disables protocol compression.

  [`0577b03`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/0577b0301689bc19d49c4081cc652971de5da8cf) Use lz4 for protocol compression.; Pull request [#2488](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2488)

  [`5c8296d`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/5c8296df066564be35d3936610ebff1c332f38da) Remove reference to disabling network compression in the documentation.
- Calculate `content-md5` on S3 only when required. (*Reviewed by David Christensen.*)

  The `content-md5` header is only calculated for batch delete requests, which require it, to save CPU when files are uploaded. This change was reverted in v2.55.1.

  Pull request [#2592](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2592); Commit [`20bfd14`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/20bfd14b73ad6d75b6fb1169565b18dcfa183c9b)
- Warn when a value for a multi-key option is overwritten. (*Reviewed by David Christensen, Stefan Fercot.*)

  Options with key/value pairs, e.g. `recovery-option`, silently overwrote a value when its key was specified again. A warning is now logged.

  Issue [#2591](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2591); Pull request [#2594](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2594); Commit [`cab4d6f`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/cab4d6fd5c775d6e1a9cd9889d825eee667b5322)
- Add detail logging for expired archive path. (*Contributed by Stefan Fercot. Reviewed by David Steele.*)

  Removing a large number of archive files could look like a hang. A message at detail level now shows progress.

  [`4dcfa9e`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/4dcfa9ef59c5e75c6c0ee83390c64b9a3d4af1fc) Add detail logging to expire test.

  [`2155a12`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/2155a12a3d861645de5618518350f90962fbc26d) Add detail logging for expired archive path.; Issue [#2543](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2543); Pull request [#2559](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2559)
- Remove support for PostgreSQL `9.4`. (*Reviewed by Stefan Fercot.*)

  PostgreSQL 9.4 is no longer supported, per the policy of supporting five end-of-life versions of PostgreSQL. Backups of 9.4 clusters can still be expired.

  Pull request [#2472](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2472); Commit [`48f511d`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/48f511dad339ef9357e00f7bd8e2bf4b7c800dd4)
- Remove autoconf/make build. (*Reviewed by David Christensen.*)

  v2.54.0 was the last release to support the autoconf/make build. Use meson to build.

  Pull request [#2470](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2470); Commit [`89c9bab`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/89c9baba72fc99616083a7adc4be2c3e2bf9e4dd)

**Documentation Improvements:**

- Fix documentation for specifying multiple stanzas with `tls-server-auth`. (*Reviewed by David Christensen, Stefan Fercot. Suggested by Terry MacAndrew.*)

  Multiple stanzas are specified for `tls-server-auth` as a comma-separated list. The documentation said to repeat the option.

  Issue [#2591](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2591); Pull request [#2595](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2595); Commit [`53a45d2`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/53a45d25c3e60e5067010b2676f5d6216638fbe3)
- Clarify incremental backup expiration. (*Reviewed by Stefan Fercot.*)

  The documentation implied that incremental backups are expired by count. It now explains how incremental backups are expired and that full backups count as differential backups for differential retention.

  Pull request [#2601](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2601); Commit [`8bdba74`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/8bdba747981e8805a9bf355050f70cdf19e9babc)
- Clarify requirement for local/remote pgBackRest versions to match. (*Contributed by Greg Clough. Reviewed by David Steele.*)

  Issue [#2540](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2540); Pull request [#2541](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2541); Commit [`82299eb`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/82299eb4cd997524eeddef170ad99669ed6651cf)
- Add FAQ about exporting self-contained cluster. (*Contributed by Stefan Fercot. Reviewed by David Steele.*)

  The FAQ explains how to create a self-contained export of a cluster with a backup made with `--archive-copy` and restored with `--type=none`.

  Pull request [#2605](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2605); Commit [`9386920`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/9386920b79971347c6c57589190bf09b591d8b4d)
- Caveat `--tablespace-map-all` regarding tablespace creation. (*Reviewed by Stefan Fercot, Christophe Courtois. Suggested by Christophe Courtois.*)

  A tablespace created after the backup starts is not in the manifest, so `tablespace-map-all` cannot map it.

  Pull request [#2538](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2538); Commit [`e625ed8`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/e625ed8be2228cb99c3801f9d1776909a2201c5a)
- Clarify behavior of `--repo-retention-full-type`. (*Reviewed by Antoine Beaupré. Suggested by Antoine Beaupré.*)

  Issue [#2562](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2562); Pull request [#2583](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2583); Commit [`84eb9a7`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/84eb9a742dcf75f071e011946263dedaa985099e)
- Change `--process-max` recommendation for object stores to `--repo-bundle`. (*Reviewed by Stefan Fercot.*)

  On object stores `repo-bundle` reduces the time spent creating files far more than `process-max` does, because fewer files are created.

  Pull request [#2542](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2542); Commit [`aa7210a`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/aa7210a1830825e0a3e6b6740937357341127a6f)
- Update `unix_socket_directory` to `unix_socket_directories`. (*Contributed by hyunkyu han. Reviewed by David Steele.*)

  `unix_socket_directory` has not been valid since PostgreSQL 9.2, so only `unix_socket_directories` is documented.

  Issue [#2589](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2589); Commit [`b28ae98`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/b28ae98cec7ae7e881e6476c1cba1ba7e1abc358)
- Recommend not placing `spool-path` within `pg_xlog`/`pg_wal`. (*Reviewed by Martín Marqués, Don Seiler. Suggested by Martín Marqués.*)

  A spool path inside `pg_xlog` or `pg_wal` can cause problems for PostgreSQL tools such as `pg_rewind`.

  Pull request [#2600](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2600); Commit [`dd3a7d9`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/dd3a7d99a78d0d604967d90e8a8dbf8915432896)

### v2.54.2 Release Notes

*Bug Fix*

*Released January 20, 2025*

**Bug Fixes:**

- Fix issue after disabling bundling with block incremental enabled. (*Reviewed by David Christensen.*)

  When bundling was disabled after backups were made with bundling and block incremental enabled, the prior backups could not be read. The setting needed to read them is now preserved.

  Pull request [#2511](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2511); Commit [`dbc6f28`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/dbc6f2875356ec16cf4883830a0d7b00204752ee)

**Documentation Improvements:**

- Clarify behavior of multiple configuration files. (*Reviewed by Paul Bierly. Suggested by Paul Bierly.*)

  Each configuration file must be valid on its own, so a section must be specified in each file that sets an option in it. The documentation also notes that quoting is not supported, whitespace is trimmed from keys and values, and repeated sections are merged.

  Commit [`5da2df4`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/5da2df4a933f04d5fb9a3b6c7c59529f3afa805b)

### v2.54.1 Release Notes

*Bug Fix*

*Released December 16, 2024*

**Bug Fixes:**

- Fix issue with `version`/`help` commands attempting to load `pgbackrest.conf`. (*Reviewed by Stefan Fercot. Reported by Bradford Boyle, Julian.*)

  In v2.54.0 the `version` and `help` commands loaded `pgbackrest.conf` and failed when it could not be read or parsed.

  Issue [#2476](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/issues/2476); Pull request [#2477](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/pull/2477); Commit [`8d69118`](https://github.com/pgbackrest/pgbackrest/commit/8d6911898afd75c586ef70c6d8559b1e5959a155)

**Test Suite Improvements:**

- Stabilize async archiving in integration tests. (*Contributed by Viktor Kurilko. Reviewed by David Steele.*)

  An async `archive-get` left running by one integration test could write an error to the queue of the next test and fail its recovery.
